# Harness Evaluation Checklist

Generated 2026-09-13 by running the `harness-eval` skill (Tech Leads Club), Track A+B+C, in one isolated run per
surface: the root context files, the `references/` folder, each `extended/<skill>/` overlay, and each local
`skills/<name>/` skill (each with its own reference files).

This is a **checklist, not a changelog**. **One row is one finding** — a file with several findings gets several
numbered rows. A surface with no findings gets exactly one `Rejected` row so its coverage stays traceable. Work
items one at a time, flip `Status` as you go, and use `Description` + `How to Fix` + `How to validate` so no row
requires re-running harness-eval or opening its run output to understand.

Raw run output lives under `.harness-eval/runs/<run-id>/` (gitignored; report-only — harness-eval never
auto-applies Ship/Slim/Mixed verdicts).

## Run notes

- **Same problem, several rows.** Each run only saw its own scope, so a cross-file problem can appear once per
  side. Fix these together: Skills #1 + #21 (architecture-evaluate Incremental vs. build-feature Step 12);
  References #2 + Skills #45 + #118 (`gh` account resolution mandatory vs. opt-in).
- **Blind-judge leak (minor).** The shell's `grep --exclude=harness-evaluation.md` did not exclude this file,
  and it held earlier runs' rows at the time. Judges in `extended/docs-writer` (C-J1), `skills/disk-evaluate`
  (C-J1, C-J2), `skills/qa-steps` (B-J2), and the `skills/not-your-babysitter` run itself saw a few lines about
  *other* scopes; each disclosed it and reported no effect on its verdicts. Future runs should exclude with
  `--exclude='*harness-evaluation.md'`.
- **Not verified live.** `skills/qa-steps`' Jira MCP tool names (no Atlassian MCP in the session), and
  `references/gh-account-resolution.md`'s public-email lookup (token read was permission-denied).

## Glossary — what each column means

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| # | Sequential reference number within its table (each table numbers independently). Use it to point at a specific item in conversation. |
| Bottom line | One-sentence statement of the finding, in plain language. |
| Description | Full narrative: what is wrong, quoted evidence, and reasoning — detailed enough that the row can be acted on without opening other files. |
| How to Fix | Concrete edit(s) that resolve the finding — what to change, where, and to what. |
| How to validate | Concrete, mechanical steps to confirm the fix resolved the finding once `Status` moves to `Done`. |
| Location | `path:line` (or `path:start-end`) where the finding lives. Several locations are listed when the finding spans files (e.g. a duplication or conflict). |
| Name | The surface this row applies to: the root context file, the `references/` file, the `extended/<skill>` overlay, or the `skills/<name>` skill. Repeats across consecutive rows when a surface has several findings. |
| Severity | `High` — causes wrong behavior, a broken step, or blocks the skill · `Medium` — likely degrades output, wastes meaningful tokens, or creates drift risk with real consumers · `Low` — cosmetic, minor token cost, optional cleanup · `—` for a no-findings row. |
| Status | `Pending` (not started) · `In Progress` (being worked) · `Done` (fix applied and validated) · `Rejected` (evaluated, consciously decided not to act — including "no findings, surface confirmed fine"). |
| Track A | Deterministic correctness check (script, ~0 model tokens): do the paths/commands the file cites exist? Highest certainty of the three tracks. `—` when not the source of this finding. |
| Track B | Redundancy check (dual LLM judges + calibration plants): would an agent rediscover this cheaply without the instruction? `Ship` only when the trap gate passed and both judges agree; a failed trap means "uncertified" — a lead, not an action on its own. `—` when not the source of this finding. |
| Track C | Usefulness check (dual LLM judges + plants + fan-in): would deleting the surface change agent behavior? `Keep-core` = essential · `Slim` = removable · `Mixed` = partly each · `Hold` = judges disagreed or trap failed. `—` when not the source of this finding. |
| Type | Category of the finding — see the list below. `—` for a no-findings row. |

**Type values:**

- `ambiguity` — two or more reasonable readings that lead to different agent behavior.
- `broken-cite` — a cited path, command, or skill doesn't exist.
- `confusing` — vague, unmeasurable, or persona-style wording the agent can't act on consistently.
- `conflict` — contradicts another instruction (same file, another skill, the base vendor skill, or the global `CLAUDE.md`).
- `deviation` — departs from the skill's own stated scope/description, the base skill's contract, or this repo's conventions.
- `duplication` — the same rule maintained in two or more harness files, creating drift risk.
- `hallucination-risk` — pushes the agent to assert, estimate, or claim something it cannot actually verify.
- `no-value` — removing it would not change agent behavior (theory, filler, or content the agent already does by default).
- `outdated` — refers to removed files/features, superseded behavior, or a base skill that has since moved.
- `redundant` — generic knowledge an agent rediscovers cheaply without the instruction.

| 2 | `CLAUDE.global.md` | Done | Medium | hallucination-risk | CLAUDE.global.md:13-15 | Clean | Review (dual KEEP-POLICY) — C005 95% threshold, C006 lead-in | Keep-core (certified, dual KEEP-CORE, trap PASS) | The 95%-confidence gate asks the model for a number it cannot measure, so the lookup it is meant to trigger fires on self-rating, not on the kind of claim. | Line 13 says `Only return a solution, suggestion, answer, tech approach, or plan if you have ≥95% confidence it is correct and appropriate`, and line 15 makes the Context7/web lookups conditional on it: `When confidence is below that threshold:`. The model has no calibrated way to put a number on its own confidence. A fact it wrongly remembers (an API signature, a flag name) feels just as certain as one remembers correctly. So the gate lets exactly the confident-but-stale claims skip verification. Taken literally, `Only return ... if` also means saying nothing when unsure, instead of answering with the uncertainty stated. That fights line 9 (`Be honest, not agreeable`). Line 21 already gives the checkable rule (`a specific version number, a recent breaking change, or an "as of X" claim usually does` need a lookup), and line 13's second sentence lists the claim types. The percentage adds nothing either rule does not already do. Both Track B judges scored C005 KEEP-POLICY, which means the section is not rediscoverable. They did not judge whether the wording is sound. In Track C, both judges scored the file KEEP-CORE and listed Confidence Threshold as behavior-changing. | Replace lines 13 and 15 with a claim-type trigger. For example: `Before stating a version number, API signature, config key or syntax, deprecation status, or migration step from memory, verify it — Context7 first, then web search. If you cannot verify it, say so explicitly and mark the claim unverified; never present an unverified value as fact.` Keep lines 16-21 as the procedure. Line 21 already explains what does not need a lookup. | `grep -n '95%' CLAUDE.global.md` returns nothing, and `grep -n 'below that threshold' CLAUDE.global.md` returns nothing. |

Two common cases are missing. (1) After context compaction or summarization, the file text is no longer in context, but the rule still says not to re-read, so the agent reconstructs contents from a summary. (2) Files change through Bash (git checkout/merge/pull, formatters, scripts), dispatched subagents, and parallel sessions. This repo had uncommitted changes from another session at the start of this run (`M docs/harness-evaluation.md`). Neither case counts as a trigger, so the agent quotes outdated line numbers or text as current. Line 82 (`After a re-read, the updated content becomes the cached version`) follows from the rest.
